#2962. Federal Reserve Board finalizes changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 9:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Wednesday finalized changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements. The two final rules are largely similar to the proposed rules from 2025. The Board also requested comment on a proposal to better capture differences in banks' business models to generate fee income.

The Board conducts stress tests to ensure that large banks are sufficiently capitalized and able to lend to households and businesses even in a severe recession. In December 2024, the Board announced it would modify the test to improve its resilience.

"The stress test is an essential component of our regulatory capital framework," said Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle W. Bowman. "Today's changes preserve its resilience by ensuring that it is transparent, granular, and risk-sensitive. The public will now have greater assurance that the risks banks take will be reflected appropriately in their stress test losses and their capital requirements."

The first final rule requires the Board to invite public input annually on the stress test scenarios and any material model changes. It also updates the framework that guides the design of the hypothetical scenarios and adopts the models that will be used for the 2027 stress test. In addition, the final rule makes several adjustments to the stress test calendar. The final rule also updates the global market shock component, which is a set of shocks to specific market components applied to banks with large trading books. These banks will now be tested against two global market shock components each year, and the Board will use the shock that produces the largest losses for each firm to calculate the firm's stress test results.

The second final rule requires the Board, when calculating stress capital buffer requirements, to average the results from the two most recent annual supervisory stress tests for firms subject to the stress test in both years. The Board will begin averaging stress capital buffer requirements in 2028 to ensure that only models incorporating public input are used in the calculation.

The proposal would request public comment on a revision to the Board's noninterest income model that would improve the stress test's ability to capture business model diversity across firms. If adopted, these adjustments would replace the current noninterest income model that projects each bank's fee income under stress. Comments are due 60 days after publication in the Federal Register.

Together, the changes are likely to reduce year-over-year volatility in capital requirements by approximately 50 percent and are not expected to materially affect aggregate capital requirements.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

Federal Register notice: Modifications to the Capital Plan Rule and Stress Capital Buffer Requirement

Federal Register notice: Enhanced Transparency and Public Accountability of the Supervisory Stress Test Models and Scenarios; Modifications to the Capital Planning and Stress Capital Buffer Requirement Rule, Enhanced Prudential Standards Rule, and Regulation LL

Federal Register notice: Request for Comment on Model Changes for the Boardâs 2027 Supervisory Stress Test

Stress Test Changes and Effective Dates (PDF)

Review of Comments and Summary of Changes to the Proposed Stress Test Models (PDF)

Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System

#2961. Wizards vs. Pistons Discussion

The Washington Wizards (4-8) are reeling amid a five-game losing streak that's featured four double-digit losses, turnover issues and stagnant offensive sets.

Washington is on the second night of a back-to-back after losing 114-101 to an Indiana Pacers squad that didn't have Tyrese Haliburton. The Wizards tallied 14 turnovers and allowed 14 offensive rebounds in an embarrassing defeat.

The Detroit Pistons (7-3) defeated Washington 122-111 on Monday at Little Caesar's Arena. Washington totaled 19 turnovers as it struggled to score against Detroit's stout perimeter defense.

WAS: Justin Champagnie (foot surgery), Tristan Vukcevic (hip impingement), Jamir Watkins (ACL tear) and Reece Beekman (AC joint) are OUT.

DET: Elijah Harkless (G League) and Isaac Jones (G league) are QUESTIONABLE. Jalen Duren (hamstring), Isaiah Joe (hip), Chaz Lanier (G League), Ebuka Okorie (G League) and Ugonna Onyenso (G League) are OUT.

#2958. NBA suspends Rockets G Marcus Smart 3 games for throwing punch at Warriors' Charles Bassey

The NBA doesn't look kindly on players who throw punches during a game. Houston Rockets guard Marcus Smart was suspended three games without pay by the NBA on Thursday after throwing a punch at Golden State Warriors center Charles Bassey during Wednesday's game.

In its statement, the league specifically says the punch made contact with Bassey's face. The suspension is set to cover Houston's games against the Utah Jazz on Friday, the Washington Wizards on Sunday and the Miami Heat on Monday.

The incident occurred in the third quarter of Houston's 115-108 win. With less than a minute to go in the quarter, Smart and Bassey appeared to exchange words on the court. Smart then threw a punch at Bassey right in front of an official.

Smart was assessed with a flagrant 2 foul and ejected from the contest. He had already exited the court by the time the officials announced the punishment.

Smart finished the game with 6 points, 2 rebounds and 1 assist.

Under NBA rules, Smart faced — at the minimum — a fine for the flagrant foul and a suspension for throwing a punch, which comes with a minimum one-game ban in the NBA rulebook:

Any player who throws a punch, whether it connects or not, has committed an unsportsmanlike act. He will be ejected following confirmation during review by instant replay and suspended for a minimum of one game.

The incident occurred after Smart and Bassey got tangled up during a rebound attempt. Following the contest, Rockets coach Ime Udoka said Smart confronted Bassey after the Warriors center wrapped up Smart's elbow on the play. Bassey reportedly told Smart "what are you gonna do," leading to the attempted punch.

Bassey, meanwhile, had something to say about what he called "fake toughness" from Smart:

"He's been playing dirty the whole game," Bassey said, according to Yahoo Sports' Kelly Iko. "I don't think he was trying to play, I think he was trying to get kicked out of the game."

Smart's absence didn't hurt the Rockets too badly in the end, as the team still managed to pull off a win. It probably helped that Warriors star Stephen Curry sustained an oblique tear early in the contest, missing much of the game.

Curry is now expected to miss some time with the oblique tear.

#2957. Top Wall Street analysts suggest these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Inflation data, earnings reports and tensions in the Middle East have been influencing the market. In this volatile backdrop, investors seeking income can bolster their portfolios by adding dividend-paying stocks.

The recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help inform investors in their search for attractive stocks from the vast universe of dividend-paying companies. These experts assign ratings after analyzing a company's financials and ability to consistently pay dividends while also seeking capital appreciation.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Exxon Mobil has increased its dividends for 43 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.03 per share (annualized dividend of $4.12), XOM stock offers a yield of 2.6%.

In his latest research report on energy players, Morgan Stanley analyst Devin McDermott reiterated a buy rating on Exxon Mobil stock and increased his price target to $177 from $168.

The analyst raised his free cash flow estimates to reflect Q2 guidance and the oil price strip, with the largest increase noted for integrated players due to higher refining margins. Notably, the five-star analyst raised his cash flow estimates by an average of 20% for the second half of 2026 and 7% for 2027.

McDermott highlighted that while pure-play refiners have rallied on stronger margins, integrated energy stocks have lagged, creating relative-value opportunities in major North American integrated producers, including Exxon Mobil, Suncor and Cenovus Energy.

Overall, McDermott is bullish on Exxon Mobil, as he expects the company to deliver strong earnings and cash flow growth, driven by robust upstream volumes, high-value projects in Product Solutions, and more than $5 billion of additional structural cost savings.

"XOM's large global integrated operations and strong balance sheet (0.0x 2027 net leverage) make it a clear defensive play in an uncertain macro backdrop," said McDermott.

McDermott ranks No. 726 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.80%. See Exxon Mobil Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Moving on to natural gas producer Expand Energy. The company announced a quarterly base dividend of $0.575 per share to be paid on Sept. 3. At an annualized dividend of $2.30 per share, EXE offers a yield of about 2.3%.

Recently, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reaffirmed a buy rating on Expand Energy stock and increased his price target to $113 from $99. Mehta attributed his higher price target to cash flow improvement and a strong outlook for shareholder returns. He also cited the stock's attractive valuation, with an 11% free cash flow, or FCF, yield based on his 2027/2028 estimates compared with the peer average of 9%.

The five-star analyst expects Expand Energy to generate FCF per share of about $10 in FY28, up from the prior estimate of about $8 at his mid-cycle price forecast of $3.50/MMBtu Henry Hub. Mehta raised his FCF estimate to reflect an improved outlook for share repurchases, operational efficiencies, and gas pricing realizations.

Furthermore, the analyst expects the recently announced $1.25 billion Twin Eagle acquisition to enhance the competitive positioning of the combined business to expand to premium markets and capture incremental deals in the power and LNG end markets.

Mehta ranks No. 449 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.1%. See Expand Energy Insider Trading on TipRanks.

Mehta is also bullish on Diamondback Energy, an independent oil and natural gas company focused on energy reserves that are primarily in the Permian Basin in West Texas. The company recently paid a base cash dividend of $1.10 per share for the second quarter of 2026. FANG stock offers a dividend yield of 2.2%.

Mehta increased his price target on FANG stock to $220 from $212 and reiterated a buy rating, citing the company's capital-efficient volume growth as oil prices remain high amid persistent supply disruption in the Middle East.

"We remain constructive on FANG, a high-quality, pure-play Permian Basin operator that continues to drive incremental capital efficiencies and strong well productivity relative to peers," said Mehta.

Also, the analyst said that the removal of a minimum return of capital commitment gives Diamondback more flexibility in taking capital allocation decisions amid high oil price volatility and macro uncertainty.

Mehta added that Diamondback sees a favorable environment for production growth, driven by the potential need for global inventory restocking. He highlighted that during Q2 2026, FANG's production volumes of 1,018 Mboe/d exceeded the high end of the company's guidance, driven by a stronger-than-expected natural gas output from Barnett development and improved downstream gas marketing.

Overall, Mehta is optimistic about Diamondback's growth and sees more upside in the stock, given higher production estimates, solid productivity and a deepening position in the Barnett shale formation. See Diamondback Energy Financials on TipRanks.

#2955. Bengals back out of trade with Eagles for Arnold Ebiketie

Bengals back out of trade with Eagles for Arnold Ebiketie originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles on Tuesday morning agreed to trade edge rusher Arnold Ebiketie to the Cincinnati Bengals for a 2027 sixth-round pick, but that deal has fallen through, a league source confirmed to NBC Sports Philadelphia.

Apparently, the trade would have canceled out a third-round compensatory pick coming back to the Bengals in 2027 after losing Trey Hendrickson to Baltimore.

Maybe Ebiketie was worth a sixth-round pick, but he certainly wasn't worth losing a third-rounder too.

Unless the Eagles find another taker for Ebiketie, he'll have to rejoin a team that just tried to trade him. The Eagles still do not have a sixth-round pick in the 2027 draft.

Ebiketie, 27, joined the Eagles this offseason on a one-year deal and has played in all nine games this season. He has played a total of 300 snaps (48%) through this point in the season. That ranks third on the team at edge rusher behind Nolan Smith (359) and Jalyx Hunt (317).

But now that Jonathan Greenard is healthier, he is expected to play more down the stretch. So the rotation for the rest of the 2026 season will include Smith, Hunt, Greenard, Ebiketie, A.J. Epenesa and rookie Keyshawn James-Newby, who has been on the practice squad. Had Ebiketie actually been traded, James-Newby was likely going to see more playing time.

Ebiketie this season has 25 tackles and 2 QB hits without a sack. He struggled against the run.

The Eagles are still projected to have nine picks in the 2027 draft:

Round 1 (own pick)Round 1 (from Jaguars)Round 2 (own pick)Round 3 (projected comp pick)Round 4 (own pick)Round 5 (own pick)Round 5 (from Ravens)Round 5 (from Patriots)Round 7 (from Cowboys)

#2948. An Eagles rookie progress report at the midway point of 2026 season

An Eagles rookie progress report at the midway point of 2026 season originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles had high expectations entering this season but are sitting at 2-7 at the bye week.

Now it's time to see what the young players can do.

The Eagles drafted eight players in April and seven of them have seen game action over the first half of the season, but to varying degrees.

With the Eagles on their Week 10 bye week, let's recap the first half of the season for all eight of their draft picks:

After some disappointing moments early in his rookie season, we have seen a ton of growth from Makai Lemon recently. Without DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks for three weeks, Lemon has had the three best games of his rookie season against the Cowboys (7 catches for 67 yards, 1 TD), Commanders (6 catches for 57 yards) and Giants (6 catches for 61 yards). While Lemon was tough on himself for a couple drops in Week 9, he has been one of the few bright spots recently.

Through nine games, Lemon has been targeted 54 times and has 37 catches for 312 yards and a touchdown. He's on pace for 102 targets, 70 catches, 589 yards and 2 touchdowns. But it's also worth noting that his numbers have been improving as the season goes on.

Here's a look at the Eagles' rookie records for receptions and receiving yards. Lemon is on pace to finish his season second on the receptions list and ninth in receiving yards. But hopefully the Eagles have scrapped all those behind-the-line passes to him.

Through nine weeks, Lemon is tied with KC Conception for the lead among rookies in receptions and is fourth in receiving yards among rookies behind Denzel Boston (507), Concepcion (437) and Carnell Tate (413).

After a rough training camp, Eli Stowers dealt with a quad injury that kept him on IR for the first six weeks of the season. He made his NFL debut a few weeks ago against the Cowboys, but hasn't played a ton on offense yet. Here's a look at his offensive snap counts for these last three weeks.

Week 7 vs. DAL: 12 snapsWeek 8 at WAS: 13 snapsWeek 9 vs. NYG: 7 snaps

Overall, Stowers has been targeted 5 times and has 3 catches for 53 yards. All three of those catches came in the loss to Washington and two of them came very late in the fourth quarter. His long of 34 yards came at the very end of the game.

But Stowers hasn't looked out of place in limited action. He has been doing some good things away from the football and has shown a real willingness to do some dirty work and block. While blocking is probably never going to be his strength, he needed to improve in that area and is clearly working on it. But in this lost season, it would make sense to see more of Stowers in the second half.

In addition to his work on offense, Stowers has also played 27 special teams snaps. That's something new for him because he began his college career as a quarterback and then became a star tight end. But he's working hard at that too.

The plan was for Markel Bell to sit all season and learn behind Jordan Mailata and Lane Johnson. But that plan got tossed in the garbage can early this season when Johnson abruptly retired. Bell was thrust into the starting lineup in Week 4 against the Rams and has started the last six games with plenty of ups and downs.

Early in his time as a starter, Bell looked really promising and looked to be settling into that role. But the last couple of games have been much rougher for the 6-foot-9 rookie. In six starts this season, according to ProFootballFocus, Bell has given up 5 sacks and 19 pressures, but 3 of those sacks came in his late-notice start against the Rams in Week 4.

There's a really good chance that veteran Fred Johnson would be a better option right now, but the Eagles should ride this out with Bell to figure out if he really has the potential to be a long-term replacement for Johnson. We'll probably learn a lot about him over the second half of this season.

Round 5-178: Cole Payton, QB, North Dakota State

The Eagles made sure to give Cole Payton plenty of reps in training camp despite having three quarterbacks on the depth chart ahead of him. But Payton has been inactive five times this season and has been active but hasn't played three times. Back in Week 5 against the Jaguars, Payton played 10 snaps on special teams and that's the only time he has seen the field.

The idea of Payton on special teams is fun, especially in a lost season like this. Maybe the Eagles will get him some more reps in that role or perhaps find a way to get him on the field on offense. Joe Milton and Tanner McKee are both still ahead of Payton on the depth chart but the way this season is going, would you be surprised to see Payton eventually get on the field as a starter?

When the Eagles drafted Micah Morris in the sixth round, he was expected to be a developmental pick and that has held true. Through 9 games, Morris has played one snap on offense and 19 on special teams. Morris has been active the last seven weeks but hasn't needed to play. If this season continues to spiral, maybe we'll get to see what Morris can do.

Round 7-244: Cole Wisniewski, S, Texas Tech

Because Cole Wisniewski missed most of training camp with injuries (hamstring and concussion), he never really had much of a shot to make the Eagles' 53-man roster. But once Wisniewski cleared waivers, the Eagles signed him to the practice squad and he has been there all season. Through nine games, Wisniewski has been elevated twice and has played 36 snaps on special teams. He has one elevation remaining.

The Eagles knew Uar Bernard was a long-term project when they drafted him and that is playing out. Bernard has next-level athleticism but is extremely raw so he needs to continue to learn the game. After the Eagles waived him at final cuts, they signed him to the practice squad. Unfortunately, Bernard suffered an injury in practice and has been on the Practice Squad Injured list back on Oct. 9. So this project is on hold for now.

Round 7-252: Keyshawn James-Newby, Edge, New Mexico

There was a lot of hand-wringing from fans in the summer when the Eagles attempted to get both Bernard and Josh Weru through waivers to the practice squad. The Eagles were probably more concerned about Keyshawn James-Newby. Because even though the seventh-round pick missed most of training camp with an ankle injury, he did flash in his preseason action. But James-Newby cleared waivers and has been on the practice squad all season.

The Eagles have used up two of his three elevations this season. James-Newby played 48 defensive snaps in his NFL debut against the Panthers and then 4 the following week against the Panthers. He has a couple of combined tackles in his defensive action. He has also played 40 special teams snaps.

The Eagles signed the 24-year-old long snapper after the draft and he has played in every game this season — totaling 77 special teams snaps. He has 3 combined tackles and a fumble recovery. He was able to show some hustle when he jumped on a muffed punt against the Giants. Underwood appears to be working well with punter Braden Mann and kicker Jake Elliott.

#2944. Pistons vs. Wizards Discussion: Game Time, TV, Odds, and More

Rarely have I been so interested in an immediate rematch as I am in tonight's game, which sees the Detroit Pistons taking on the Washington Wizards for the second consecutive contest. The Pistons prevailed in Monday's game 122-111. The game was also just dang weird. Anthony Davis played out of his mind, scoring 44 points in just 34 minutes. If instead the Wizards bounce back, it could get messy.

#2939. Cavs former All-Star to miss matchup with Giannis Antetokounmpo and Heat

Cleveland Cavaliers power forward Evan Mobley will miss Friday night's game against the Miami Heat due to a sprained big toe, according to the team's official injury report. Mobley suffered the injury late in Wednesday's game in New York when he went up for a block and took a hard fall.

While there is never an opportune time for an injury, losing a key piece of the front court ahead of a date with the Miami Heat will be difficult to overcome. Giannis Antetokounmpo and Bam Adebayo make for a very formidable front court duo, and having Mobley would have helped counter them immensely. Jarrett Allen, who recently returned from an injury of his own, will be tasked with securing the interior on defense next to Mario Hezonja or Peyton Watson.

Anything bought from the links helps support Fear the Sword. You can buy the Believeland shirt HERE. You can also shop all of Homage's Cavs gear HERE.

Mobley played 33 minutes on Wednesday, finishing with 10 points, five rebounds, four assists, and one block. He shot 4-of-8 from the floor, but had an overall quiet stat line.

Cleveland has multiple candidates that could fill in for Mobley on Friday.

The Cavs started Tyrese Proctor for a game with Allen out, and could opt to do the same with Mobley not available. Watson, who had a very nice game defensively against the Knicks, may slide over to power forward to make room for Proctor. Jaylon Tyson, who also returned from injury, is another candidate to start. Mario Hezonja's size may make him a more ideal candidate for additional playing time, though he had a mostly forgettable game on Wednesday.

Mobley has generally fared well on defense against Antetokounmpo, relative to the rest of the league. Giannis will still dominate games and impact winning, but Mobley has been able to use his size and quickness to at least be a deterrent. The Cavs will not have that Friday night, making it an even more daunting task to slow down Antetokounmpo and the Heat.

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2936. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 stocks for the long haul

Global stock markets have been volatile as investors assess high bond yields and continued tensions between the U.S. and Iran. There are concerns about the impact of uncertainty in the Middle East on oil prices and consequently on inflation.

Investors who prefer to look beyond short-term noise and buy stocks with attractive long-term growth potential can follow the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts. The ratings of these experts are backed by in-depth research of a company's financials and thorough analysis of the factors affecting its business performance.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Semiconductor giant Nvidia (NVDA) reported stellar fiscal second-quarter results and reassured investors about continued strength in AI-driven demand for its chips. Moreover, Nvidia's FY28 revenue growth outlook of 70% was well above the Street's expectations.

Reacting to the Q2 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reiterated a buy rating on NVDA and increased his price target to $300 from $288. "This is our Top Pick in the semis group, with a compelling product cycle, exceptional growth and valuation below peers," said the 5-star analyst.

Nvidia delivered impressive quarterly results despite supply constraints, Moore said, but he believes that the FY28 revenue and gross margin commentary mattered more, with a favorable outlook for both measures.

Moore said Nvidia's 70% revenue growth guidance for FY28 was above his own estimate of 52% and the consensus of about 40%. The top-line growth estimate was a "remarkable figure" given supply issues and Moore said he expects Nvidia to continue to address bottlenecks to deliver rapid growth.

Among other takeaways, the analyst said performance metrics for Vera Rubin chips were strong, with Nvidia emphasizing a 30x higher throughput per megawatt and 35x lower token cost compared with Grace Blackwell Ultra.

Moore ranks No. 159 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 24.60%. See Nvidia Statistics on TipRanks.

Ride-hailing and delivery platform Uber Technologies (UBER) was recommended by BMO Capital analyst Brian Pitz, who discussed the company's opportunity in the autonomous vehicle (AV) market in a recent research note that reiterated a buy rating and price target of $119.

The 5-star analyst highlighted Uber's transition into a diversified marketplace, with investors now focusing on its AV growth prospects. The key question is how much value can Uber generate as AVs become commercialized, Pitz asked.

"We continue to believe that Uber's AV strategy is evolving and will become a significant driver of revenue and profitability," the analyst said.

Importantly, Pitz believes that Uber can establish itself as a preferred mobility platform for AV makers, supported by its growing AV infrastructure capabilities and an expanding partner base. This would enable Uber to win a significant share of value as the AV market is commercialized.

Additionally, Pitz noted Uber's enhanced position in the AV value chain, with the company shifting from serving as a distribution channel for robotaxis to becoming a broader platform for AV mobility. The analyst discussed Uber's efforts to capture growth opportunities in the AV market, including higher capital spending, expansion into new markets and building partnerships beyond Alphabet's Waymo.

Pitz ranks No. 574 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of about 12.20%. See Uber Technologies Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Semiconductor company Marvell Technology (MRVL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the second quarter of FY27. But the company's increased FY28 guidance fell short of investors' high expectations.

In reaction to the Q2 FY27 results, KeyBanc analyst John Vinh reaffirmed a buy rating on Marvell Technology, with a price target of $400. The analyst noted that Marvell's results and outlook were driven by strength in its data center business. The Q2 FY27 data center business grew 46% year-over-year, to $2.17 billion, far above KeyBanc's estimate of $2.09 billion.

Management "highlighted accelerating AI networking demand across scale-out, scale-across and scale-up architectures, supported by strong 800G optical DSP demand, rapid 1.6T ramp, broader 51.2T switch deployments and higher TIA/driver demand," Vinh said.

Consequently, Marvell raised its data center revenue guidance, now expecting 60% growth in FY27 and more than 60% in FY28.

Regarding the recently announced $120 billion warrant arrangement with Google, Vinh said management argued that programs tied to the warrant are already priced into the custom outlook through FY28. Meanwhile, new programs and those in execution are expected to drive significant upside to the previous FY29 custom XPU revenue estimate of over $10 billion, Vinh said, citing management.

Vinh ranks No. 123 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 28.50%. See Marvell Technology Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2934. The next step for the Eagles at DT after trading Jordan Davis

The next step for the Eagles at DT after trading Jordan Davis originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles made a massive trade on Monday, shipping Jordan Davis to the Jacksonville Jaguars for a 2027 first-round pick and a 2029 second-round pick.

After this Davis trade, it suddenly makes sense why Eagles general manager Howie Roseman reportedly kept telling teams that he wasn't looking to move Ojomo before Tuesday's trade deadline. In fact, he was telling them he wanted to re-sign him. That should now be the next step.

While the Eagles clearly made this Davis trade because they got excellent return, it does open the door to pairing Ojomo with Jalen Carter long-term in the middle of this defense.

The Eagles used a seventh-round pick to take Ojomo out of Texas back in 2023 and he has become a very good NFL player. But when the Eagles extended both Carter (4 years, $152 million) and Davis (3 years, $78 million) this offseason, it looked likely that Ojomo would play out his rookie contract and leave in free agency.

Ojomo seemed destined to follow the path of former Eagles DT Milton Williams, who played out his rookie deal before signing with the Patriots before the 2025 season.

But with Davis gone, Ojomo has a real chance to stay and the Eagles should want to keep him. He's a 25-year-old homegrown talent with pass rush ability at a premium position.

In recent seasons, Roseman hasn't handed out in-season extensions because he hasn't wanted to create locker room divides that could hurt a Super Bowl push. Roseman won't have to worry about that in 2026, so he shouldn't hesitate to extend Ojomo if a deal is possible.

Back in the summer, Ojomo said there had been discussions between the team and his agent.

And as recently as a couple weeks ago, Ojomo still thought it was possible he could stay in Philly. That was obviously before the Davis trade.

"I don't think any door is shut," Ojomo said before the Washington game. "I wouldn't say that. But you don't know what the future holds."

Last year, Ojomo had a breakout season. He played in all 17 games, made 9 starts and had a six-sack season. Even though he doesn't have a sack yet in 2026, he has still been disruptive. In fact, he has the highest pressure rate of any Eagles' interior defensive lineman and the highest of his career, per NextGen Stats.

And while pressure rate isn't the only thing that matters and there are other variables like attention from opposing offensive lines, how's this for comparison: All-Pro Jeffery Simmons has a pressure rate of 10.2% this year, just ahead of Ojomo at 10.1%.

Here's a look at Ojomo's pressure numbers by season:

2023: 0 sacks, 2 pressures, 5.4% pressure rate2024: 0 sacks, 5 QB hits, 23 pressures, 9.1% pressure rate2025: 6 sacks, 12 QB hits, 6 TFLs, 29 pressures, 6.9% pressure rate2026: 0 sacks, 5 QB hits, 4 TFLs, 22 pressures, 10.1% pressure rate

So while Ojomo hasn't been able to finish at the quarterback, he has 22 pressures in 9 games after having 29 in 17 games in his breakout season. Those numbers at least tell you that Ojomo has been disruptive. And he has to trust that the sacks will follow.

It's not easy to figure out what Ojomo's next contract will look like but there are 20 defensive tackles in the NFL who are making $20+ million annually. Carter tops that list at $38 million, but Davis and Williams are in that list at $26 million. Ojomo is probably going to join that group or at least get close.

The only difference is that now it's more likely to happen in Philly.

"Howie (Roseman) and (Jeffrey) Lurie mean so much to me for giving me this opportunity to play for this team," Ojomo said a couple weeks ago. "I think I was the 23rd DT picked up. If we can make it work, I love playing for this city, I love working hard for this city. I want to produce results for this city."

#2931. Mike Brown not ready to make a Mikal Bridges role change despite offensive struggles

Mikal Bridges is basically a starter in name only at the moment.

His minutes and production both resemble a bench player so far this season. It's really only his inclusion in the pregame introductions that represents his status as a starter.

But Knicks coach Mike Brown is not yet ready to make a change.

"Mikal is a starter, so it's not a question of that," Brown said. "Like a lot of players, they've had their good moments and their not-so-good moments. Mikal has a proven track record of playing at a pretty high level as a starter in this league. So for me to make a quote-unquote panic decision and change it, right now we're [11] games in or whatever we are, I'm not feeling it."

New York Knicks guard Mikal Bridges (25) reaches for the ball during the first quarter against the Cleveland Cavaliers, Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026, in New York. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

Bridges played just 17 minutes in the Knicks' 115-107 loss to the Cavs Wednesday night at Madison Square Garden and only recorded two points. He was on the bench for the entirety of the fourth quarter.

For the season, Bridges is averaging 24.4 minutes per game — notably fewer than Landry Shamet's 25.9 minutes per game despite Shamet coming off the bench eight of the Knicks' 11 games.

Bridges' offensive struggles to start the year have resulted in Brown increasingly opting to go with Shamet or Miles McBride on the court, particularly to close games. Shamet and McBride have played 69 and 43 fourth-quarter minutes this year, respectively, compared to Bridges' 41. Bridges' 5.9 points per game are eighth most on the team, and he is shooting a woeful 32.9 percent from the field and 26.5 percent from 3-point range.

He has scored just four total points in fourth quarters this year — fewer than bench-warmers Pacôme Dadiet and James Wiseman, who usually only play in garbage time, have produced. It's half of what Dalton Knecht, who has played in just three games this year, has delivered in fourth quarters.

Brown's changing sentiment is telling. He began the year saying that his starting five was entrenched and that everyone else on the roster was fighting for minutes. Then, last week, he said that Shamet is "about as close to being a starter as you can imagine for us." And after Wednesday's game, Brown admitted that he feels a need to make room for Shamet and McBride.

New York Knicks head coach Mike Brown reacts on the baseline during the second quarter of an NBA game against the Milwaukee Bucks on Tuesday, Nov. 10, 2026. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

Bridges can usually at least hang his hat on the defensive end, but that's an area in which Shamet and McBride are thriving as well. Brown has praised their point-of-attack defense and said that their tracking data has shown that Shamet has been their best pick-and-roll defender this season.

"We got two guys that are playing really well in Landry and Deuce," Brown said. "So it's hard. It's gonna be hard to find minutes at times for Mikal. And maybe even at times for Josh [Hart], because those two guys are playing at a pretty high level. Not just on the offensive end of the floor, but defensively too. … Landry and Deuce have been a big part of what we've done defensively. They're playing at a high level. I gotta keep trying to find ways to get those guys on the floor."

The Knicks have certainly been here before with Bridges. He went through significant slumps each of the past two regular seasons before coming up big in the postseason.

Last year, it reached a crescendo during the first round of the playoffs, when the Knicks fell to a 2-1 series deficit to the Hawks. Brown repeatedly sat Bridges in the second halves of those first three games and faced constant questions about replacing him in the starting lineup.

But Brown held firm with Bridges, who rewarded him by playing a key role in the championship run.

The Knicks are confident a similar bounce back is looming.

"No matter what's going on, with what people are saying or anything, the people in this room have his back night in and night out," Jalen Brunson said. "We see the work he puts into his game. We see the time that he puts in. When you're doing the right things day in and day out, things will eventually fall in your favor. So just giving him support, giving him confidence every single day, regardless of what the noise is on the outside. That's our brother, we're gonna keep it that way."

#2927. International player Joshua Weru on his development behind the scenes

International player Joshua Weru on his development behind the scenes originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Joshua Weru was the talk of training camp this summer. While draft pick Uar Bernard grabbed headlines this spring, it was the Eagles' other International Player Pathway Program player who began to stand out in August.

The edge rusher from Kenya flashed in practices and even in the preseason a bit — to the point where some worried he'd be claimed if the Eagles dared to waive him to get him to the practice squad.

But that was months ago and now we're in November.

Weru has spent all season on the Eagles' practice squad. And most days this season, Weru walks through the Eagles locker room mostly unnoticed. He'll sit at his locker without being bothered and hadn't really been asked for an interview all season. Until last week. So Weru seemed a bit surprised when he was approached and asked for a few minutes, which he graciously handed over.

What have the last couple of months been like?

"It's been good," Weru said. "I continue to say I'm so lucky to be here. The amount of reps we get on scout team and developmental have been good. Just seeing things better and recognizing blocks quicker. Just getting my strike stronger. Even though I'm not necessarily getting stronger in the weight room, like actual game strength is getting slightly better. Just those things because you're more used to doing certain movements so you're getting better at it."

Weru, 23, hasn't been elevated yet this season so he hasn't played in a game since the preseason. Weru misses playing, but last week invoked the old proverb — absence makes the heart grow fonder — to explain his situation.

Over the last couple months, Weru knows he has gotten better. Although he's unsure how to quantify it, his growth is undeniable.

Away from the spotlight of training camp, Weru has settled into a rhythm as a practice squad player. Tuesdays are big for him to get in extra voluntary work. And then on days the Eagles practice, he goes out early to get in what Nick Sirianni always calls "500 shots" of extra work. Those extra sessions for Weru and fellow rookie Keyshawn James-Newby are run by outside linebackers coach Jeremiah Washburn and assistant linebackers coach Tyler Scudder.

But a big part of Weru's role as a special teams player is contributing on the scout team. So each week, he dons a pinny and tries to give the Eagles' offensive line the best looks he can for that upcoming game.

Jordan Mailata has seen a lot of Weru on the scout team this season and admitted the rookie got under his skin a little bit to end last week: "He's very fast and twitchy so he gave a great look this week."

Mailata admitted that because of Weru's lack of football knowledge, misdirections will fool him to where he'll over commit or lose contain in the run game. But Mailata has already seen Weru improve in those areas week by week.

"He's going through that but he's doing a great job of not taking that too personal," Mailata said. "He's hard on himself a good amount. His effort is greater than mine ever was. I just saw him chase down Saquon (Barkley) it was incredible. Incredible."

Weru takes pride in his job as a scout team player and keeps a close eye on the Eagles' offensive linemen on Sunday.

"That gets me fired up, getting to watch them," Weru said. "If I see that they've been doing well, I feel like I helped a bit at least."

The interesting part of being a scout team player for someone like Weru, who hasn't been playing football for very long, is that it exposes him to other styles of defense. This year on scout team, Weru has been learning how to play out of three-point and four-point stances, which has taken some getting used to.

Weru said he didn't realize how hard it would be to get out of a stance like that.

"I feel like I'm slowly getting better at that," he said. "It's also helped me get more reactive because it's not playing to the rules that we have. So you kind of have to know what's going on."

Weru has been practicing every week, while his fellow IPP teammate, Bernard, has been on the Practice Squad Injured List since Oct. 9.

Bernard and Weru were big stories back in training camp and many wondered if they would make the 53-man roster and feared if they didn't, another team would scoop them up. But both players were cut, cleared waivers and immediately signed to the Eagles' practice squad.

While Weru obviously wanted to make the Eagles' roster, he never wanted to leave. And now that he's away from the spotlight a bit, he has been able to grow each week behind the scenes.

No one really knows what the future holds for Weru. Even though the Eagles once hit it big on an IPP player in Mailata, the odds are stacked against Weru and Bernard. But there's no harm in trying and Weru has continued to put in the work.

"It's been really good because just growing and building relationships with guys in my room, coaches, the development staff, all my teammates," Weru said. "So I just feel increasingly comfortable. I can see how much time they're willing to invest in me. I'm just happy to be here."

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

Warriors center Charles Bassey kept his hands down when Marcus Smart swung at him.

The Houston Rockets guard will now sit for three games after the NBA suspended him Thursday for throwing a punch that made contact with the Golden State Warriors center's face.

Golden State Warriors center Charles Bassey, left, and Houston Rockets guard Marcus Smart, center, react during an NBA game in Houston, Nov. 11, 2026. AP Photo/Ashley Landis

Smart turned and threw a punch that grazed Bassey's nose. Officials assessed a Flagrant 2, and Smart walked off before they announced the ejection.

Golden State Warriors center Charles Bassey, center left, is held back after a confrontation with Houston Rockets guard Marcus Smart, center, Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026. AP Photo/Ashley Landis

The league's decision draws a line under the altercation, but it won't change the result for Golden State. Bassey did not retaliate, and the confrontation ended without a larger fight or additional ejections.

Brandin Podziemski #2 of the Golden State Warriors drives to the basket against Marcus Smart #36 of the Houston Rockets on Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026. Getty Images

It was already a costly night for the Warriors: Stephen Curry left in the first quarter with right abdominal soreness, and Golden State's eight-game winning streak ended with the defeat.

Bassey's composure kept the incident from compounding the loss in the moment; Smart's suspension means he will watch Houston's next three games from the sidelines.

#2921. Top Wall Street analysts recommend these 3 dividend stocks for higher returns

Dividend-paying stocks can enhance portfolio returns by providing a steady income stream to investors and the potential for capital appreciation. Several investors seek exposure to dividend stocks in a volatile backdrop to ensure stable income.

In this regard, recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, backed by strong financials and cash flows to support consistent dividend payments.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Energy Transfer (ET) is a diversified midstream energy company with about 140,000 miles of pipelines and associated energy infrastructure in 44 states. The company made a quarterly cash distribution of 34 cents per common unit for the second quarter of 2026. At an annualized payment of $1.36 per common unit, ET offers a yield of 6.3%.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Jeremy Tonet reiterated a buy rating on Energy Transfer stock and slightly raised his price target to $25 from $24. The analyst noted that the company delivered a strong Q2 beat across all key metrics.

Tonet highlighted that Energy Transfer raised its 2026 adjusted EBITDA guidance to $18.8 billion-$19.1 billion from $18.2 billion-$18.6 billion, reflecting robust base results and cost optimization. The 5-star analyst added that the company "tightened" its capex to $5.6 billion–$5.9 billion and expects capex above $5 billion annually through 2029, given solid opportunities.

About the Hugh Brinson pipeline, Tonet noted Phase 1 (1.5 bcfd) reaching full capacity by September 1 and Phase II capacity (0.7 bcfd) going online by Q1 2027. The analyst also noted completion of a 14-mile Hugh Brinson lateral in Abilene, Texas, and final stages of talks for an additional 250 mmcfd of Oklahoma power plant demand.

"All in, we see ET well positioned to continue capitalizing on organic growth opportunities across the value chain," said Tonet.

Tonet ranks No. 922 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 9%. See Energy Transfer Financials on TipRanks.

This week's second dividend pick is independent oil and natural gas company Permian Resources (PR). The company declared a base dividend of $0.16 per share for the third quarter, payable on September 30, 2026. At an annualized dividend of $0.64 per share, PR offers a yield of about 2.7%.

Recently, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Permian Resources stock and increased his price target to $27 from $22, citing an improved outlook on oil production growth and operational efficiencies.

The 5-star analyst noted that while PR stock has outperformed its peers so far in 2026, he sees further upside. Mehta is confident about the company's operational improvements and ability to deliver incremental free cash flow per share through the strategic ground game program. He added that the program has completed about $1.05 billion in bolt-on deals this year (as of August 5).

"We see the continued success of PR's ground game as constructive toward future inventory replenishment and increasing working interest over time," said Mehta.

The analyst also emphasized Permian Resources' solid track record of generating growth in free cash flow (FCF) per share. Mehta expects the company's free cash flow per share to grow at a 20% CAGR (compound annual growth rate) from 2025–2028.

Mehta ranks No. 401 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.8%. See Permian Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

Moving on to Sempra Energy (SRE), a utility holding company. Earlier this month, the company declared a quarterly dividend of $0.6575 per share, payable on October 15. At an annualized dividend of $2.63 per share, SRE stock offers a yield of about 3.1%.

Recently, Jefferies analyst Julien Dumoulin-Smith upgraded SRE stock to Buy from Hold but slightly lowered the price target to $97 from $101. The 5-star analyst noted that Sempra stock is trading at a price/earnings multiple that is 14% below its electric peers due to concerns about Texas transmission capital expenditure and the failure of California legislation.

Smith noted that the derating of weaker California peers PG&E (PCG) and Edison International (EIX) is weighing on the sum-of-the-parts (SOTP) valuation but is becoming less relevant given their materially higher fire risk compared to Sempra Energy.

Furthermore, Smith expects Sempra Energy's Texas capex plan to stay in place despite the opposition to transmission and data centers, though delays to 765-kV projects are likely. While a full recovery for SRE stock will need a clearer outcome from the Texas legislative session, Smith believes that the stock may already be an early opportunity for patient investors.

"We are early on the upgrade but see limited downside at current levels as the market is pricing in little transmission upside," said Smith.

Smith ranks No. 945 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 8.2%. See Sempra ETF Exposure on TipRanks.

#2918. Eagles snap counts: What happens after Jordan Davis trade?

Eagles snap counts: What happens after Jordan Davis trade?  originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

In his final game as a Philadelphia Eagle, Jordan Davis logged 38 snaps (57%) against the Giants in Sunday's loss. As he has been for most of the season, Davis was third among the Eagles' defensive tackles in playing time.

Davis on Monday was traded to the Jacksonville Jaguars for a 2027 first-round pick and a 2029 second-round pick.

Here's how those DT snaps broke down on Sunday in the 19-11 loss to the Giants:

Jalen Carter: 58 snaps (87%)Moro Ojomo: 42 snaps (63%)Jordan Davis: 38 snaps (57%)Byron Young: 16 snaps (24%)Ty Robinson: 9 snaps (13%)

In the first half of the season, Davis was third among the defensive tackles in snaps at 52%, while Carter has played 84% and Ojomo has played 60%. Davis played a total of 323 snaps in nine games, which is an average of around 36 snaps per game. Those snaps will need to be made up by others.

It's hard to imagine Carter playing more than he already has been this season and maybe Ojomo could play a bit more. But it seems like Young and Robinson will definitely see more playing time. That is intriguing with Robinson, who is a second-year fourth-round pick who has played just 61 defensive snaps all season. It's time to see what the young defensive tackle can do and whether or not he has a chance to be in the rotation in 2027.

The Eagles also have Gabe Hall and Zion Wilson on the practice squad. It's possible we see one or both of them at times the rest of 2026.

• The Eagles' defense played 67 snaps and Cooper DeJean, Drew Mukuba and Quinyon Mitchell played all of them. The defense was good on Sunday but Mukuba didn't have his best game.

• Zack Baun played 32 snaps (48%) before he left the game with a back injury. Before that, Baun was all over the field; he had 7 tackles and 2 sacks. Jeremiah Trotter Jr. played 64 snaps (96%) and Smael Mondon Jr. played 26 snaps (39%). Remember, Jihaad Campbell missed this game with injuries.

• At edge rusher, Nolan Smith led the way with 44 snaps (66%), followed by Jonathan Greenard – 36 snaps (54%), Jalyx Hunt – 29 snaps (43%) and Arnold Ebiketie – 25 snaps (37%). Greenard got his first sack of the season and Smith got his fifth.

• Without Michael Carter II (abdomen), Marcus Epps took back his safety job and played 43 snaps. Kenny Moore was the dime player with 7 snaps.

• Joe Milton made his first NFL start. Milton and his full offensive line played 70 snaps. Milton was sacked seven times by a team that entered the game with nine all season.

• Saquon Barkley (hamstring/back) looked a little healthier on Sunday but it didn't help. He played 41 of 70 snaps (59%) but had just 22 yards on 13 carries. Elijah Mitchell played 29 snaps (41%) and made some bad mistakes in the fourth quarter. Jaydon Blue was the third running back and didn't see any time on offense.

• Without DeVonta Smith (hamstring) and Dontayvion Wicks (ankle) for the third straight game, both Makai Lemon and Darius Cooper played 65 of 70 snaps (93%), followed by Hollywood Brown – 35 snaps (50%) and Elijah Moore – 26 snaps (37%). Lemon led the team with 6 catches for 61 yards but he also had a couple of costly drops.

• At tight end, Dallas Goedert played 57 of 70 snaps (81%), followed by Johnny Mundt – 8 snaps (11%), Grant Calcaterra – 7 snaps (10%) and Eli Stowers – 7 snaps (10%). It'll be interesting to see if Goedert gets moved today. Either way, Stowers needs to play more going forward.

• The Eagles used Fred Johnson as an extra blocker on 8 snaps and used Willie Lampkin as a fullback on 2 snaps. Let's see some more of Lampkin. Why not?

#2917. LeBron James out, Joel Embiid available; Adem Bona seeing a specialist

Update, 6:30 p.m.: Joel Embiid has been upgraded to available.

Update, 1 p.m.: LeBron James has officially been ruled out with low back/sciatica management.

The Toronto Raptors have ruled out Scottie Barnes (left groin strain) and Trayce Jackson-Davis (illness). Kawhi Leonard remains out with a left knee injury.

As the Philadelphia 76ers gear up for tonight's home game against the Toronto Raptors, we received a good sign on the availability of two of their stars. Joel Embiid and LeBron James both participated in morning shootaround after being placed on the injury report as questionable yesterday evening. Embiid even spoke to reporters, but could not provide any further information about whether he will suit up tonight.

Nevertheless, you'd rather see the two of them out there than not. The Sixers could certainly use their two largest starters against Toronto, particularly with the handful of absences already in the frontcourt.

On that note, Dominick Barlow also participated in shootaround, but there is still no timeline for his return. We'll await word closer to game time on the availability of Embiid and James. Either way, expect plenty of minutes for Dean Wade and Jabari Walker. Stay tuned.

#2911. Roob's Stats: Makai Lemon shows up again and 2nd-half defense doesn't

Roob's Stats: Makai Lemon shows up again and 2nd-half defense doesn't originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

It's pretty amazing that a team that's lost seven straight games can produce any positive stats.

We've been doing this Roob's Eagles Stats column for five years now, so we've never had to deal with last-place records, long losing streaks or blowout losses. But welcome to the 2026 Eagles.

When they're good, the stats just write themselves. Now? Not so much. You really have to be creative to come up with a few.

But thankfully we have Makai Lemon, Braden Mann, Zack Baun, Dallas Goedert and a few others to start with before we move on to the reality that this is one of the worst Eagles teams ever.

MORE MAKAI LEMON: This is becoming a weekly tradition. Because Lemon has been one of this team's few bright spots. With six more catches for 61 yards, Makai Lemon increased his season totals to 37 catches for 312 yards. He already has the 8th-most catches by an Eagles rookie in a season, and he's now tied with the Browns' K.C. Concepcion for most catches this year by a rookie. Lemon's 27 receptions in the last five games are 2nd-most ever by an Eagles rookie in any five-game span and most in 86 years. In 1940, Don Looney had 28 catches in games against the Dodgers, Steelers, Lions, Steelers and Washington.

BAUN WITH THE WIND: With his second two-sack game, Zack Baun became only the fifth Eagles linebacker with multiple games with multiple sacks. Seth Joyner had four, William Thomas three and Baun and Garry Cobb had two each. In all, Baun has nine sacks as an Eagle, 6th-most all-time by an Eagles linebacker behind Joyner (37), Thomas (34), Mychal Kendricks (14), Jeremiah Trotter (12) and Cobb (12). No Eagles linebacker has had more than 4.0 sacks in a season since Willie T. had 5.0 in 1997.

OUR FIRST JOE MILTON STAT: Welcome to Roob's Eagles Stats, Joe! Milton's 24-yard run in the fourth quarter matches the longest by an Eagles quarterback in his first career start in 62 years. Late in 1964, the Eagles beat the Cowboys 24-14 at Franklin Field, and Eagles QB Jack Concannon had a 29-yard run in his first start. Jalen Hurts also had a 24-yard run in his first career start, late in 2020 against the Saints at the Linc.

GOEDERT PILING UP THE STATS: With five catches for 50 yards, Dallas Goedert increased his career totals to 440 catches for 4,984 yards. With 16 yards Sunday against the Steelers, Goedert will become the 11th Eagle with 5,000 yards and another 35 yards will move him past A.J. Brown and into the all-time Eagles top-10. Goedert also needs 13 catches to pass Pete Retzlaff and move into third place in Eagles history in receptions behind only Harold Carmichael (589) and Zach Ertz (582). With 60 catches, Goedert will be the 22nd tight end with 500 receptions. And with 16 yards he'll become the 45th tight end with 5,000 yards.

RUN DEFENSE FOR A CHANGE: Devin Singletary ran 21 times for 31 yards Sunday, and his 1.5 rushing average is 3rd-lowest ever against the Eagles on 20 or more carries. In 2016, Jeremy Hill of the Bengals was 23-for-33 (1.4 average) in a 32-14 Bengals win at Paul Brown Stadium and in 1993 Neal Anderson ran 20 times for 24 yards (1.2 average) in a 17-6 Bears win over the Eagles at the Linc. Singletary's average was lowest by a Giants running back on 20 or more carries in 75 years, since Eddie Price was 20-for-15 for 0.8 yards per carry in a 37-31 win over the New York Yanks in 1951 at the Polo Grounds.

LET'S TALK ABOUT BRADEN MANN'S PUNT (the good one): We won't talk about Braden Mann's negative-two-yard punt in the fourth quarter after he couldn't handle Rocco Underwood's snap. But despite that punt, Mann still averaged 48.0 yards per punt Sunday, thanks in great part to his historic 86-yard punt just before halftime. Mann punted from the Eagles' 8-yard-line down to the Giants' 6, and that is the 2nd-longest punt in Eagles history. The only longer punt was by Randall Cunningham, who sailed a 91-yarder over Dave Meggett's head against the Giants at the Meadowlands in 1989. Mann's 86-yarder is tied for 10th-longest in NFL history and was the longest in 25 years, since Rodney Williams of the Giants had a 90-yarder against the Broncos at altitude at Mile High Stadium from the Giants' 10-yard-line into the end zone. So that was only a 70-yard net. Mann's 86-yarder is the longest not at altitude since 1991, when Shawn McCarthy of the Patriots had a 93-yarder down to the Bills' 1-yard-line at Rich Stadium. Mann also had two punts inside the 20 and no touchbacks. Through nine games, Mann has 22 punts inside the 20 and two touchbacks. In four years with the Eagles he has 67 inside the 20 and just 18 touchbacks.

THE ROCCO UNDERWOOD STAT: Rocco Underwood's fumble recovery of a muffed punt by Giants returner Charlie Jones was the first fumble recovery by an Eagles long snapper in 22 years. On the final day of the 2004 Super Bowl season, in a meaningless game against the Bengals, Mike Bartrum recovered his own fumble after catching a pass from Jeff Blake.

LONGEST STREAK EVER: The Eagles have allowed 10 or more points in the second half of their first nine games for the first time in franchise history. Their longest previous streak of games to start a season allowing double-digit points after halftime was seven in a row in 2020. Going back to last year, the Eagles have allowed 10 or more 2nd-half points in 11 straight games, most in franchise history. They had streaks of nine straight games over the 1972 and 1973 seasons and the 1994 and 1995 seasons. The longest previous streak of double-digit second halves in the same season was seven in a row in 1967, 2012, 2015 and 2020.

FIRST TIME IN 50 YEARS: The Eagles have been outgained by over 100 yards per game during their seven-game losing streak, the first time they've been outgained by over 100 yards per game during any seven-game span in 50 years. The seven teams the Eagles have faced during the losing streak have out-gained the Eagles by 912 yards – 2,595-1,683. That's the most yards the Eagles have been outgained by in a seven-game span since 1976, when they were outgained by 976 yards from Week 5 through Week 11 by the Cards, Packers, Vikings, Giants, Cards, Browns and Raiders. The Eagles have also been outscored by eight or more points in each of their last four games, the first time that's happened since 2012 and only the third since 1976.

THE EAGLES HAVE NEVER BEEN THIS BAD IN THE SECOND HALF: After getting outscored 13-3 in the second half Sunday, the Eagles have been outscored 126-40 this year after halftime, and that 86-point 2nd-half scoring differential is the largest in franchise history through nine games. The previous largest point differential through nine games was 81 in both 1971 and 1972. Only the Ravens have been outscored by more points in the second half – an incredible 96 points. The Eagles' 86-point 2nd-half deficit is 11th-largest by any NFL team in the last 40 years. The Eagles' 4th-quarter deficit of 58 points (85-27) is also largest in franchise history, breaking the previous record of 51 set in 1935, the franchise's third year of existence. The 85 points the Eagles have allowed in the fourth quarter is also most in franchise history, breaking the previous high of 84 set in 1973. Their opponents' 126 2nd-half points is 4th-most in franchise history and most since the 1995 team allowed 129.

THERE'S MORE! The Eagles' 13 points in the third quarter so far is their fewest through nine games since 1999, when they scored just 10, and their 27 points in the fourth quarter is also fewest since 1999, when they scored 26. So naturally their 40 total 2nd-half points is fewest since 1999, when they had 36, which you could have already figured out. Since 1942, the only other year the Eagles scored as few as 40 2nd-half points through nine games was 1972, when they managed just 26.

EVERYBODY GETS A SACK: The Giants had seven sacks Sunday and the Eagles had six, and that made this only the fourth game in Eagles history where both teams had at least six sacks. In 1984, the Falcons beat the Eagles 26-10 at Fulton County Stadium, and the Falcons had eight sacks and the Eagles nine. In 1986, the Eagles beat the Raiders in overtime at L.A. Coliseum, with the Eagles recording six sacks and the Raiders 11. And in 2023, in a loss to the Saints at the Linc, the Eagles had seven sacks and the Saints had six. The seven sacks were the most by any team against the Eagles since 2020, when the Washington Football Team had eight and the most by the Giants against the Eagles since they had 12 in the Winston Justice Game at the Meadowlands in 2007. It was the most sacks the Eagles have allowed at home in 23 years, since the Patriots had eight in a 31-10 win at the Linc early in the 2003 season, and the most the Giants have had in Philly since they had nine in a game at the Vet in 1997.

COLE PAYTON, ANYBODY? Eagles quarterbacks have had a passer rating below 80 in all seven games during the losing streak. That's Jalen Hurts against the Bears, Rams and Jaguars, Tanner McKee vs. the Panthers, Cowboys and Commanders and Joe Milton against the Giants. This is the first time since 2006 Eagles QBs have gone seven straight games without a passer rating of 80. In 2006, the Eagles had nine straight sub-80 passer ratings – one by Donovan McNabb and seven by Mike McMahon. Before that it last happened in 1971.

'DOES ANYBODY REMEMBER INTERCEPTIONS?' The Eagles don't have an interception in their last four games, the 4th-longest streak in franchise history without an INT. They went eight straight games in 2012, six in a row in 1976 and five in a row in 2020. They've faced 133 passes since Quinyon Mitchell picked off Trevor Lawrence in London in the third quarter in Week 5. The Eagles have been held without an INT seven times in their first nine games. The most games in a season they've been held without an INT is 11 in 2012.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2910. Without Steph Curry, Warriors rookie is going to be put to ultimate test

Yaxel Lendeborg has never lacked confidence.

When the Warriors were in the running to acquire LeBron James, the rookie expressed excitement about potentially playing alongside one of the greatest players of all time but also told the California Post, "Hopefully he doesn't take away my minutes."

And after Lendeborg and Luka Doncic exchanged trash talk when the Lakers played the Warriors in their season opener, Lendeborg said he "lost all respect" for one of the NBA's biggest superstars, calling out his "whining" and "complaining."

After Steph Curry suffered an oblique abdominal tear in the Warriors' 115-108 loss to the Rockets on Wednesday, Lendeborg is now the team's star.

Curry will be reevaluated in two weeks and is expected to be sidelined close to a month, according to ESPN, meaning it's now up to the 24-year-old to carry the franchise.

And so far he has walked the walk, averaging 21.7 points, 7.4 rebounds and 3.7 assists while surging to the top of the Rookie of the Year race.

That's a different challenge altogether. We're really about to see what Lendeborg is capable of, especially considering Curry had opened the season playing out of his mind.

The 38-year-old Curry was having an MVP-caliber campaign, entering Wednesday's game averaging 30.6 points, 4.3 assists and 4.1 rebounds for the Warriors, who had stunned the league by winning eight straight games.

Golden State Warriors player Steph Curry attends the season opener against the Los Angeles Lakers in Los Angeles on Wednesday, Oct. 21, 2026. London Entertainment

Overnight, Lendeborg has gone from being a sidekick to the main character.

Draymond Green said he already saw glimmers of that happening Wednesday.

"It's funny because the thing that he's going to have to learn and understand is, all right, Steph wasn't out there, KD (Kevin Durant) was off the court, you look around, you're probably the best player on the floor," Green told reporters. "Regardless of team.

"I think there was a point he realized, ding, ding, ding, and he started attacking, and the game flipped for us. I think that's something that he's going to have to realize really fast. There will be oftentimes where he can possibly be the best player on the floor regardless of the team. We need him to show that. He doesn't have a problem doing it."

Lendeborg has been one of the season's biggest stories.

Golden State Warriors player Yaxel Lendeborg, left, and head coach Steve Kerr smile during a game against the Houston Rockets on Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

The Warriors' 11th pick in the draft breathed new life into Curry's career during what was thought to be a lost season after Jimmy Butler and Moses Moody suffered injuries that would sideline them for the first few months and Kristaps Porzingis announced he was stepping away from basketball indefinitely.

Lendeborg picked up the slack. Brandin Podziemski is making major contributions, averaging career highs in points (17.3), field-goal percentage (48.1) and 3-point shooting (39.7%). And Green is capable of bottling up anyone in the league on any given night.

The person who's going to feel that most is Lendeborg, who's going to experience a different type of defensive pressure than he's ever seen.

Curry is a four-time MVP. He carried the Warriors to four championships in eight years from 2015-2022 before the dynasty began flailing, failing to advance past the second round of the playoffs the last four years.

Lendeborg helped resuscitate Golden State. Now he's going to have to put it on his shoulders.

Lendeborg wants that. He wants to be a star. But can he handle it now?

Golden State Warriors forward Yaxel Lendeborg (1) shoots against Houston Rockets forward Kevin Durant (7) in Houston, Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026. AP Photo/Ashley Landis

Green made waves at the top of the season for saying the Warriors found the "next face of the franchise" in Lendeborg. That meant a lot coming from Green, who doesn't give that type of praise easily, especially to a rookie.

We're going to get a preview of whether he was right. Whether Lendeborg is ready. Whether he's able to handle the pressure.

Last season, the Warriors went 13-26 without Curry, who missed 27 straight games because of runner's knee.

They're better equipped to deal with Curry's absence this time around because of an outspoken and talented rookie who's about to be thrust into a situation he was supposed to be eased into.

For however long Curry is out, Lendeborg is the face of the Warriors.

His confidence is going to be put to the ultimate test.

#2906. Top Wall Street analysts find these 3 stocks attractive as long-term investments

Investors have been grappling with stock market volatility, weighing elevated oil prices, persistent geopolitical tensions, the Federal Reserve's rate hike and a debate about AI safety. For all that, it helps to look beyond short-term noise and focus on stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors gain clarity, as these experts conduct in-depth research to pick stocks with attractive growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Enterprise software and cloud infrastructure company Oracle (ORCL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027. The quarter benefited from solid growth in cloud infrastructure revenue.

After hosting a series of investor meetings with Ken Bond, Oracle head of investor relations following Q1 earnings, Guggenheim analyst John DiFucci reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $400. Oracle remains a Guggenheim Best Idea, he said.

"We continue to believe ORCL is a decade stock, driven by the massive (and profitable) opportunity ahead for AI training and inferencing," said DiFucci. He also cited the traditional Public Cloud business and additional AI-enabled cloud database and cloud applications for his bullish stance. DiFucci expects all the factors to drive revenue and profit acceleration over the next few years.

While Oracle delivered 850 MW of data center capacity in Q1 FY27, Bond cautioned that Infrastructure as a Service revenue in any quarter depends more on the timing of when that capacity comes online, than on the headline MW number. DiFucci expects IaaS growth to stay strong, with full-year growth expected above Q1 FY27's 120% constant currency rate. Management expects total revenue growth to accelerate sequentially throughout fiscal 2027, as the faster-growing infrastructure business makes up a larger share of the mix.

DiFucci addressed concerns regarding customer concentration, saying OpenAI's share of total remaining performance obligations has dropped from 80% last June to about 50% now, and is expected to keep falling as new contracts are signed with new and existing customers.

DiFucci ranks No. 263 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 62% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17.5%. See Oracle Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

Space systems and launch services provider Rocket Lab (RKLB) was recently initiated as a buy with an $80 price target by Raymond James analyst Brian Gesuale.

"RKLB has assembled launch, spacecraft, components, payloads and optical communications, with Neutron and Iridium completing medium-lift and applications/spectrum in 2027," said Gesuale.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that with the completion of the Iridium acquisition, Rocket Lab will become the only vertically integrated space company with positive free cash flow (FCF). The deal will add a deployed constellation valued at more than $3 billion, globally coordinated L-band spectrum and about $500 million in earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) and $300 million in free cash flow (FCF).

Gesuale highlighted RKLB's strong revenue and backlog growth and progress toward positive EBITDA and FCF in 2027/2028. Rocket Lab is moving from a heavy investment phase into monetization, with estimated revenue growth of 59% in 2026, the analyst said, noting that RKLB's backlog has more than doubled to almost $2.4 billion from $1.1 billion in Q3 2025.

Given strong growth projections, the analyst expects RKLB's gross margins to expand from about a 35% trough in 2027 to about 47% by 2030, while R&D spending is estimated to decline from ~35% of sales in 2026 to about 10% in 2028, creating substantial operating leverage as the platform matures.

Gesuale ranks No. 369 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17%. See Rocket Lab Statistics on TipRanks.

J.P. Morgan analyst Doug Anmuth recently upgraded Meta Platforms (META) to buy from hold and raised his price target to $820 from $640. Despite the year-to-date underperformance of META, the 5-star analyst still sees significant upside, as Meta is in the early stages of launching frontier models and AI-powered offerings beyond advertising, particularly the Muse AI agent and Meta Model API access.

"Frontier models are at the core of Meta's product and monetization pipeline over a multi-year period, and its path toward superintelligence," said Anmuth.

In the summer of 2025, Meta set a goal to rebuild its Meta Superintelligence Labs team and deliver frontier AI models within a year, the analyst said. The team appears to have met its goal, rapidly advancing from the rollout of Muse Spark 1.1 in July to Muse Spark 1.3, which is competitive with Claude and GPT models, Anmuth said.

Meanwhile, Meta's Watermelon product will expand opportunities in consumer products, business intelligence, and Family of Apps engagement, the analyst said. The product will enhance internal operations and overall efficiency, said Anmuth, who's confident of Meta's ability to bring consumer-driven AI products to its extensive user base of 4 billion and views its distribution network as a competitive advantage.

While Meta has focused on frontier models and AI product development, Anmuth also sees solid opportunity in its AI infrastructure capacity, which he believes will support future compute needs.

Anmuth ranks No. 832 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of about 9.40%. See Meta Platforms Financials on TipRanks.
